# G8 — soak (60 min, release build)

**Verdict: PASS** — run 2026-10-19T23:26:33.469Z, 60 min, commit `28ce4a0`, `target/release/deckhand`, Darwin 25.6.0 arm64, 15 × Apple M5 Pro, 24 GiB.

Generated by `scripts/gates/soak.sh` (driver `scripts/gates/soak.mjs`, fake agent `scripts/gates/soak.agent.cjs`); rerun it to regenerate this file.

## Setup

- Sandbox: one `mkdtemp` directory in `$TMPDIR` holding `DECKHAND_HOME`, `HOME`, a fresh git repository and a private copy of the binary; removed afterwards. `~/.deckhand`, `~/.codex`, `~/.claude` untouched; the real agents never run (`PATH` starts with the fake one).
- 5 sessions: soak-1 (claude, worktree none), soak-2 (claude, worktree new), soak-3 (codex, worktree none), soak-4 (pi, worktree new), soak-5 (claude, worktree none).
- Fake agent: 12 cycles of 300 s — busy 5–195 s (16 colored 256-color lines with background, a cursor-save/up/clear/restore status line every 40 ms, a full-screen redraw every 3 s, absolute cursor jumps, two 1 MiB bursts per session per cycle at staggered times, PreToolUse/PostToolUse hooks every 25 s, UserPromptSubmit at the start and Stop at the end, all through `$TEST_CLI hook`), then idle until the next cycle (no output, one timer).
- Subscriber: one protocol connection for the whole run: `subscribe` to the repository, and during busy stretches it switches `watch-preview`/`watch-git`/`watch-terminal` between sessions every 15 s (the protocol side of switching tabs).
- Churn client: a second connection that, during busy stretches, `attach`es to the next session every 10 s (resizing every other time) and `detach`es.
- UI client: `deckhand` in a real PTY (node-pty, 130×36) launched in even cycles and quit with `q` 100 s into the following odd cycle (so half of the idle stretches have a UI attached); every 12 s of busy time it selects a session (1–5), switches tabs (Tab, `]`, `[`, `t`, `g`, `a`, `p`), scrolls the preview, or opens the agent full screen (Enter) and returns (Ctrl+]). 6 launches, 228 keys, 46.0 MiB of UI output.
- Sampling every 30 s (`ps -A -o pid,ppid,pgid,stat,rss,time,command`, `lsof -n -P -p` counting numeric fds) over the daemon's and the harness's process trees and every process running the binary: RSS, macOS `phys_footprint` (`footprint --pid`), CPU time (CPU % = CPU-time delta / wall-time delta of the interval), open fds, state, the members of its process group (`group_procs`: the daemon's group holds the daemon and its workers; an agent leads its own) and its descendants (a session worker's: its agent).
- Phases: an interval is `idle` when it lies entirely inside an idle stretch (the first idle interval starts 15 s after the agents stop), `busy` when entirely inside a busy one, `mixed` otherwise. Warm-up: the first 2 cycles (10 min); "after warm-up" = minutes 10–20, "end" = the last 10 min.

## Traffic and stop

- Subscriber events: {"session-updated":281,"agent-versions-updated":5,"preview-updated":27503,"git-updated":7,"terminal-updated":27}; hook signals seen (attention events): {"SessionStart":5,"UserPromptSubmit":63,"Stop":60}.
- Churn client events: {"attached":213,"output":179556,"detached":213}; 136.8 MiB of agent output received while attached.
- Sessions at the end: [{"title":"soak-1","status":"running","attention":"response-ended","event":"Stop"},{"title":"soak-2","status":"running","attention":"response-ended","event":"Stop"},{"title":"soak-3","status":"running","attention":"response-ended","event":"Stop"},{"title":"soak-4","status":"running","attention":"response-ended","event":"Stop"},{"title":"soak-5","status":"running","attention":"response-ended","event":"Stop"}].
- Stop: daemon exit {"code":0,"signal":null,"ms":116}; leftover processes []; files left [].

## Reading (hand-written; not regenerated)

- **Memory:** after every busy stretch the daemon goes back to 10.4–10.6 MiB RSS and every session worker to 6.1–6.6 MiB.
  The values at the end of each idle stretch are flat from cycle 0 to cycle 11 (see the table above). Busy-time RSS
  peaks of 35–53 MiB per worker come from the 1 MiB bursts and the terminal model, and they are given back each time.
  The median RSS of the last 10 minutes is lower than in the 10 minutes after warm-up for the daemon (−2.6%) and for
  every worker (−19% to −26%). The idle-sample trend is within ±0.1 MiB/h. `phys_footprint` medians move by −6% to
  +14% between the two windows, in both directions. That is noise from when the bursts land relative to the
  samples, not growth.
- The fake agents (node) are the only processes whose footprint rose (+30–35%). They are part of the harness, not
  the software under test, and their RSS fell slightly.
- **fds:** the daemon holds 34–37 fds while idle: 34 with only the subscriber and churn connections, 36–37 when
  the workspace workers and the UI's connections exist. It settles at 37 with a UI and 36 without one in every cycle
  after the first. Each session worker holds 11 fds in every sample.
- **Idle CPU:** over the fully idle intervals, the daemon, the session workers and the workspace workers use
  0.00–0.03% of a core, with or without the UI attached. The UI itself uses at most 0.03%.
- **Phase classification:** a sample lands a few milliseconds after its nominal time. The interval ending on a cycle
  boundary (270–300 s) therefore counts as `mixed`, so each cycle has 2 fully idle intervals instead of 3. This is
  conservative: no interval with agent output is ever counted as idle.
- **Churn refusals:** 3 `attach` requests were refused with "session is already attached elsewhere". Each happened
  while the PTY UI held that same session full screen. An agent attach is exclusive, so this is expected.
